# Proposed OEIS comments: A270724 to A282310

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A270724

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A270724>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [8810](../papers/8501-9000/8810-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: (n+2)*a(n) +(-n-2)*a(n-1) +(-7*n+6)*a(n-2) +10*a(n-3) +(13*n-32)*a(n-4) +(5*n-32)*a(n-5) +(-11*n+52)*a(n-6) +4*(-n+6)*a(n-7) +4*(n-7)*a(n-8)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Oct 07 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 6, so the recurrence holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A272706

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A272706>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [9205](../papers/9001-9500/9205-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: Rule 523 also generates this sequence. - _Lars Blomberg_, Jul 08 2016
> Conjecture: a(n) = 3*4^n - 8*2^n + 9. - _Lars Blomberg_, Jul 08 2016
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jul 08 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured closed form is correct. The entry states a generating function as fact; a linear recurrence with polynomial coefficients follows from it exactly, the conjectured form satisfies the same recurrence, and the two agree at enough consecutive indices to be equal from then on. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A273019

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A273019>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [9223](../papers/9001-9500/9223-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: +(241*n+56) *(2*n+1) *(n+1)*a(n) +(482*n^3-25561*n^2+13831*n-56) *a(n-1) +(-48682*n^3+225897*n^2-300131*n+125310) *a(n-2) +9*(n-2) *(2651*n-3183) *(2*n-3) *a(n-3)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, May 16 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A273351

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A273351>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [8884](../papers/8501-9000/8884-PROOF.pdf), [8978](../papers/8501-9000/8978-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*(13*n-40)*a(n) +(-55*n^2+211*n-129)*a(n-1) +(38*n^2-212*n+255)*a(n-2) +(-6*n^2+56*n-75)*a(n-3) +(13*n^2-66*n+3)*a(n-4) -(3*n-13)*(n-6)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 06 2016
> Conjecture: n*(n-3)*(n-2)^2*a(n) -(n-3)*(2*n-3)*(2*n^2-6*n+3) *a(n-1) +(2*n^4-16*n^3+41*n^2-36*n+5) *a(n-2) +2*(n-1)*(2*n-5) *a(n-3) +(n-2)*(n-5)*(n-1)^2 *a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 06 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A273676

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A273676>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [8797](../papers/8501-9000/8797-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: Rule 849 also generates this sequence. - _Lars Blomberg_, Jul 23 2016
> Conjecture: a(n) = A270222(n) for n>1. - _R. J. Mathar_, May 30 2016
> Conjecture: a(n) = 3*4^n - 4*2^n + 1, n>1. - _Lars Blomberg_, Jul 23 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This identity is true. Both sides have a generating function that the entries themselves record, and after clearing denominators the difference is identically zero, which is a finite polynomial check. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A274723

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A274723>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2687](../papers/2501-3000/2687-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + 4*a(n-2) + a(n-3) - 3*a(n-4) - 4*a(n-5) for n>7.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(5 + 9*x - 24*x^2 - 45*x^3 - 21*x^4 - 4*x^5 + 8*x^6) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - 4*x)*(1 + x + x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 30 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 155 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) - 9*a(n-2) + 10*a(n-3) - 6*a(n-4) + a(n-5) for n>6.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(6 - 16*x + 22*x^2 - 20*x^3 + 8*x^4 - x^5) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 4*x + 5*x^2 - 5*x^3 + x^4)). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 31 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 223 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A278023

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A278023>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [8909](../papers/8501-9000/8909-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: +n*(3*n^2-12*n+11) *a(n) -(3*n-5) *(3*n^2-9*n+4) *a(n-1) -2*(2*n-5) *(3*n^2-6*n+2) *a(n-2) +n *(3*n^2-12*n+11) *a(n-3) -2 *(2*n-5) *(3*n^2-6*n+2) *a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 24 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 4, so the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A278472

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A278472>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [8885](../papers/8501-9000/8885-PROOF.pdf), [9057](../papers/9001-9500/9057-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: +(n+2)*(n^2-4*n+1)*a(n) +2*(-4*n^3+9*n^2+16*n-6)*a(n-1) +(15*n^3-38*n^2-9*n+14)*a(n-2) +2*(2*n-1)*(n^2-2*n-2)*a(n-3)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 06 2017
> Conjecture: +(n+2)*a(n) +(-11*n-14)*a(n-1) +(37*n+20)*a(n-2) +(-25*n-6)*a(n-3) +2*(-21*n+41)*a(n-4) +4*(-2*n+5)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 06 2017

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 2, so the recurrence holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [602](../papers/0501-1000/0602-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) -6*a(n-2) +6*a(n-3) -7*a(n-4) +8*a(n-5) -9*a(n-6) +8*a(n-7) -5*a(n-8) +4*a(n-9) -3*a(n-10) +2*a(n-11) -a(n-12) for n>19.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 2 050 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 19. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A281473

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A281473>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2456](../papers/2001-2500/2456-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) - a(n-2) + a(n-3) - a(n-4) + a(n-5) -a (n-6) for n>11.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(16 + 232*x - 379*x^2 + 189*x^3 - 251*x^4 + 124*x^5 - 288*x^6 + 132*x^7 - 24*x^8 + x^9 + 2*x^10) / ((1 - x)*(1 - x - x^3 - x^5)). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 19 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 561 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
